# Independent source-grounded rhetorical review

Read your literal execution plan separately and the complete allowed reader, including every document and shared block, in bounded untruncated output. Confirm the sole absolute output and await controller authenticated reading release before authoring. No network, other debates, peer reviews, existing tags, scores, winners, session logs, environment, credentials, paid services or agents. The checker is hash-pinned executable input; do not read its implementation.

## Current source restrictions

Paul Copan defends the Canaanite conquest as morally compatible with a loving God; Randal Rauser contests the moral adequacy of that defense. Both are Christians. The motion and speaker positions are frozen in the source packet. This is a substantive moderated discussion with final statements, not formal timed rounds; no audience Q&A occurs.

Assess only the retained discussion within approximately 4:31–1:25:19. Follow the complete frozen scope restrictions. Host arguments and their dependent replies are excluded, notably approximately 55:53–1:04:05 and 1:07:46–1:09:29, as are introductions, breaks and moderator commentary. No credit, penalty, quotation or silence inference may rely on those exclusions.

All 18 moves and five semantic sections are frozen, with nine moves per side. Seventeen below-high-confidence audio checks are complete. The source-lock’s caption-only state is historical; the completed audio findings below qualify it. No direct listening is claimed. Secondary automatic transcription verifies eligible inferences but does not authorize rewriting exact canonical quotations. Copan’s unclear pottery terminology and possible Moses/Jesus verbal substitution remain ineligible as independent premises, quotations or penalties; uncertain name spelling and isolated numbering slips do not count against either speaker.

Rauser grants hyperbole and Copan’s nuance; his remaining issue is civilian targeting and cultural removal, not a demand that every totalizing phrase be literal. Copan permits limited contextual exceptions but rejects some divine commands and calls conquest unique and unrepeatable. His airplane analogy concerns foreseen innocent deaths, which must not be silently equated with direct targeting. Rauser’s alternatives are a survey, not a claim to have proven one interpretation. His future-openness quotation belongs to another scholar, not Copan.


Tags describe only material conduct present in the recoverable source and already reflected in the frozen critique. They never alter ratings. Use only the exact local catalog definitions, not nearby informal meanings. Contested premises, incomplete mechanisms, weak analogies and failure to exclude alternatives do not automatically establish circularity, ignorance, equivocation or any other tag. Citation alone is not an appeal to authority. Two tags for one defect require two distinct catalog-defined failures. Zero accepted tags is legitimate; do not invent candidates to meet a quota.

For a blind-review packet, output exactly {schemaVersion:"1.0-source-grounded-rhetorical-review",debateNumber,debateId,reviewedMoveIds,moveReviews,candidateReviews,acceptedTags,audit}. reviewedMoveIds and moveReviews follow the source packet’s final publication order exactly. Each moveReviews item is {moveId,reason}, with at least 22 words identifying the actual inference or qualification and explaining why a plausible definition applies or why the real limitation is not a catalog-defined defect. Write each explanation individually; substitutions of names or roles in generic no-tag prose do not count. candidateReviews includes every serious candidate considered, accepted or rejected, once as {moveId,type,label,decision,rationale}; rationale is at least 20 source-specific words. Accepted rows additionally have context of 8–35 words. acceptedTags is the accepted subset in move order, as {moveId,type,label,url,context,rationale}, with exact catalog URL and identical context/rationale. audit contains exactly completeSourceReviewed, allMovesReviewed, otherReviewerUnavailable, scoresUnavailable, sourceRestrictionsRespected, noScoreChanges, all true only if actually satisfied.

For an anonymous-adjudication packet, read every union candidate and the entire source. Output exactly {schemaVersion:"1.0-source-grounded-rhetorical-adjudication",debateNumber,debateId,decisions,audit}. decisions contains every packet candidate exactly once in its order as {moveId,type,label,decision,rationale}, adding context only for acceptance. Decide each independently from the exact definition, complete source and frozen critique; do not vote-count or add candidates. Rationales are at least 20 words; accepted contexts are 8–35 words. audit contains exactly completeSourceReviewed, allCandidatesReviewed, passIdentitiesUnavailable, scoresUnavailable, sourceRestrictionsRespected, noScoreChanges, all true only if satisfied. The packet determines the complete union length; never substitute the move count.

After reading release, construct exact UTF-8 JSON in persistent memory only. Run the pinned checker with explicit --debate and --packet through stdin. Resolve unsaved mechanical issues within this same authorized context, then send the actual existing bytes via stdin to the maintained workflow handoff hold using your plan. Keep the holder alive and report its hash/size receipt. Do not save drafts, write your own output or approval, overwrite an output, restart the context, or replace held bytes without controller review. The controller must inspect every explanation including no-tag decisions before authorizing one exact-byte first save.

# Frozen source instructions

Motion: Is the Canaanite conquest morally defensible as an act commanded by a loving God?

Assess only Copan and Rauser within the frozen window and exclusions. This is a moderated conversation with substantive introductory cases and final statements, not formal timed rounds. No audience Q&A occurs.

All enumerated host arguments and dependent replies are unscored: approximately 55:53–1:02:11, with the break excluded through 1:04:05, and Rauser’s correction of host framing at 1:08:49–1:09:29. No credit, penalty, quotation, reply link or silence inference may rely on them.

Ordinary questions and faithful recaps are context only, never a selected speaker’s evidence. The host explicitly attributes the 45:36–46:17 coercive-force summary to Copan and the 1:13:06–1:13:59 judgment question to Copan. Both lines are independently present in the immediately preceding primary speech; these prompts add no independent evidential argument.

Copan and Rauser both affirm Christianity and divine inspiration. Copan defends a historically grounded, hyperbolic conquest as compatible with divine love; Rauser criticizes civilian killing and the adequacy of that defense while offering alternative readings. Do not impose atheism, rejection of all scripture, or proof of Christianity’s falsity on Rauser.

Copan accepts hyperbole and historical limits, does not assert that every textual total means every person died, distinguishes ideology from ethnicity, and permits contextual moral exceptions in his closing. Do not attribute the more sweeping genocide defenses of other authors to him.

Rauser explicitly concedes exaggeration and the legitimacy of several Copan insights. His challenge is that reduced numbers or cultural removal can leave a moral problem; he does not claim that recognizing any hyperbole is itself dishonest or that historical uncertainty proves all events fictitious.

Rauser’s reply at 1:07:46–1:08:49 refers to Copan’s excluded collateral-harm answer. That dependent subargument may not be selected or used to judge Copan. Earlier independent challenges and the later independent closing claims remain eligible; no alleged unanswered objection may depend on the excluded answer.

The source contains automatic-caption errors in proper names, Marcionite, Christocentric hermeneutic, herem/haram, Ai, Hazor, Kenites, Jude references and atemporal. Do not penalize or silently amend those words in exact quotations. Treat each uncertain selected material wording/reference as below high and require audio verification before adjudication.

The spoken biblical, archaeological, scholarly, modern legal and historical claims are assertions made in the debate, not externally verified facts. Do not import the publisher article, outside scripture text or outside scholarship into judgment.

At 44:13–44:20 Copan immediately corrects Acts 13 to Acts 12. At 50:59–51:12 he challenges the alleged Nazi comparison. Preserve such corrections and distinctions; short mixed turns are not automatically attributable.

No direct audio listening or second independent transcript has occurred. Every selected move with material wording or speaker uncertainty must be below high with a precise audioVerificationReason.
